                    This appendix retains the economic-inference, safeguarded-selection,
7                                                                                                  7
                 localization, certified-readout, deployment, recursive-query and vector-
8                                                                                                  8
                 control sections without textual deletion. It records their original scientific
9                evidence and verification boundaries. Complete numerical catalogues re-           9

10               main alongside the exact qualification duals. The new evidence section            10

11               reports the prespecified independent calibration/evaluation, all paired in-       11
                 tervals and width components, price-fee breakpoints, and exact multi-date
12                                                                                                 12
                 regression examples. Core proofs remain in the Technical Supplement; the
13                                                                                                 13
                 original broader applications retain their unchanged companion editions.

     APPENDIX A: F ROZEN NEURAL CONTINUATIONS AND POLICY- COST PRECISION
16                                                                                                 16

17     The optimal-advantage estimator in Section 1 makes a policy comparison informa- 17
18   tive, but its conditional optimal-value queries are expensive. A fitted continuation          18

19   can replace those queries without changing the estimand. The necessary certificate            19

20   concerns its Bellman residual across states, not its distance from an unknown op- 20
21   timizer. This places the original NBO continuation, rather than only a proposed               21

22   action, inside the evaluation calculation.                                                    22

23                                                                                                 23

30       The terminal correction in (1) is essential unless h T = g. Conditional expectation,            30

31   not pathwise equality with the realized cash-cost difference, supplies (2). The result              31

32   uses the actual kernels and costs; it assumes neither a correct statistical transition              32

1    model estimated from data nor a convex neural fit. The pointwise policy certificate      1

2    also applies conditionally after an adaptive choice among valid continuations. The       2

3    independent-sample confidence statement below requires its additional sampling           3

4    conditions.                                                                              4

5      For M conditionally fixed comparisons, suppose independent trajectory rows             5

6    yield outward enclosures [ Li , Ui ] of Dh , intersected with its valid support. Write   6

7    Rh = 2Ωh + Hhπ + Hhν . A simultaneous interval with failure probability at most α is     7

8                      "            r                           r             #               8

10                                                                                            10

11   This follows by the bounded-sum inequality applied to both endpoint samples and          11

12   a union bound. The numerical and sampling width allocations of Corollary 2 apply         12

13   with Rh . Fitting, uniform residual qualification, conditional integration and any       13

14   subsequent selection are distinct paid operations. In particular, a cache that adapts    14

15   across rows changes the controller’s history: its rows cannot be treated as indepen- 15
16   dent evaluations of one frozen Markov policy. A fixed cache or an independent            16

17   episode-reset design is sufficient to retain the stated sampling construction.           17

19                 A.2. Conditional integration of an actual neural continuation              19

20                                                                                            20
       A shallow rational affine–ReLU continuation has the form
21                                                                                            21
                                                       K
22                             h(y) = c + ℓ⊤ y + ∑ v j (w⊤
                                                         j y + u j )+ .
                                                                                              22

23                                                    j =1                                    23

24   Its readout weights may have either sign. In the original investment laws the next       24

25   state, conditional on ( x, a), is affine in one or two independent centered uniform      25

26   innovations. A neuron therefore has projected center z and nonnegative projection        26

27   radii A, B. Write z+ = max{0, z}.                                                        27

28                                                                                            28

29     P ROPOSITION 2—Exact conditional ReLU moments: For independent uniform                 29

30   projections of radii A, B > 0, the conditional means of a neuron are                     30

                            (z + A)2+ − (z − A)2+

4                                                                                                      4
     A zero projection radius is removed; if all radii vanish the mean is z+ . Consequently P a h is
5                                                                                                      5
     a finite exact rational calculation for rational primitives, states, actions and weights. For n
6                                                                                                      6
     trajectories and at most K neurons per date, its arithmetic-operation count is O(nTKd),
7                                                                                                      7
     exclusive of fitting, trajectory construction and uniform qualification. No optimal-value
8                                                                                                      8
     descendant query occurs in this calculation.
9                                                                                                      9

10   The proof is in Supplement, Section 5.2.                                                          10

11       The operation count does not bound rational bit complexity. Near-zero projected               11

12   radii can also make a naive floating evaluation unstable; directed rounding or                    12

13   rational evaluation, including the zero-radius branches, is required. The executed                13

14   moment catalogue checks the rational identities against an independently written                  14

15   polygon integral. It is a conditional-integration experiment, not a new trained-policy            15

16   cost comparison. The formulas do not imply that the achieved residual oscillation                 16

17   of an arbitrary fitted network is small.                                                          17

19                  A.3. Qualifying a fitted continuation without a Bellman tree                       19

20
         Uniform qualification need not query V ∗ . Suppose Qth is Lipschitz in state and              20

21
     action with constants L x,t , L a,t , and ht has constant Lh,t , all in ℓ1 . In the investment    21

22                                                                                                     22
     capacity simplex, an action feasible at y can be repaired continuously at x with
23                                                                                                     23
     displacement at most κ ∥ x − y∥1 , where κ = 1/(8d). Thus a valid residual Lipschitz
24                                                                                                     24
     constant is
25                                                                                                     25
                                       Lr,t = L x,t + κL a,t + Lh,t .
26                                                                                                     26

27   Let a finite state cover have fill radius ρ, and let [l j , u j ] enclose rth at every cover      27

28   point. Then                                                                                       28

29                                                                                                     29
                              min l j − Lr,t ρ ≤ rth ( x ) ≤ max u j + Lr,t ρ                    (8)
30                              j                             j                                        30

31   throughout the cube. At a cover point, a feasible action mesh of fill radius s gives              31

32   a lower infimum bound by subtracting L a,t s from the smallest action-value lower                 32
                                NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                              5

1    endpoint; the smallest upper endpoint is an upper bound. Exact moments can                      1

2    supply those action endpoints. None of these steps requires convexity of h or a                 2

3    true-value curvature minorant for Qh .                                                          3

4      For Nt state-cover points and At actions per point, this qualification costs                  4

5    O(∑t Nt At Kt+1 d) arithmetic operations, plus certification of the moduli and any              5

6    actor-specific greedy-gap bound. Its cover can still be expensive in high dimension. 6
7    The result replaces a recursive optimal-value oracle by an explicit approximation- 7
8    and-qualification tradeoff; it does not make that qualification free. The residual- 8
9    cover proof, the action-mesh argument and the terminal allowance are given in the               9

10   supplement. Value-function control variates have an established variance-reduction              10

11   literature (Munos, 2006); the additional requirement here is an original-law, whole- 11
12   domain certificate for the fitted NBO continuation and the actual acquired policy.              12

14             APPENDIX B: F ITTING A CONTINUATION FOR QUALIFICATION                                 14

15
       A continuation used to evaluate a policy should be fitted to the error that de- 15
16                                                                                                   16
     termines the evaluation cost. The residual-cover bound in (8) supplies such an
17                                                                                                   17
     objective. We now make its optimization constructive. The future continuation is
18                                                                                                   18
     held fixed at each backward step; neither an optimal-value oracle nor success of
19                                                                                                   19
     unrestricted hidden-layer training is assumed.
20                                                                                                   20
       Fix a date and suppress its subscript. Let x1 , . . . , x N be a state cover of radius ρ in
21
     the ℓ1 metric, and suppose                                                                      21

22                                                                                                   22

23                                      y−                     +
                                         j ≤ (T h+ )( x j ) ≤ y j .                                  23

24                                                                                                   24
     These are verified bounds on the original continuous-action infimum, not only
25                                                                                                   25
     on a proposal menu. Write hθ = ∑kK=1 θk ϕk , where the fixed features have verified
26                                                                                                   26
     Lipschitz constants Lk and |θk | ≤ Bk . The constant feature is allowed with Lk = 0.
27                                                                                                   27
     Let C be a verified Lipschitz constant of T h+ , including state-dependent feasibility
28                                                                                                   28
     repair. With Fjk = ϕk ( x j ), define

1        T HEOREM 3—Qualification-optimal readout: For every feasible θ, osc(T h+ −                   1

2    hθ ) ≤ W (θ ). Minimizing (9) is a finite linear program with 2K + 2 variables and 2N + 2K       2

3    inequality constraints, apart from variable bounds. For probability vectors p, q on the nodes,   3

4    set λk = 2ρLk and                                                                                4

5                                                                                                     5

6                                                                                                     6
                D( p, q) = p⊤ y+ − q⊤ y− + 2ρC − ∑ Bk |[ F ⊤ (q − p)]k | − λk + .
                                                                             
                                                                                              (10)
7                                                      k                                              7

8                                                                                                     8

9                                                                                                     9
     Then D( p, q) ≤ infθ W (θ ), with equality for some p, q. Thus any feasible readout and exact
10                                                                                                    10
     probability vectors certify an achieved optimization gap of at most W (θ ) − D( p, q).
11                                                                                                    11

12                                                                                                    12

13       The proof is in the Technical Supplement. It constructs the primal constraints               13

14   and dual support function explicitly. Rationalizing a solver’s proposed readout                  14

15   and probability vectors gives a checkable certificate; a convergence flag is not the             15

16   certificate. Label intervals, features, moduli, coefficient bounds and the cover remain          16

17   part of the numerical contract.                                                                  17

18       Applying the theorem backwards gives a fitted continuation at every date. Its                18

19   achieved ranges Wt , together with the terminal oscillation and the actual imple- 19
20   mented greedy-gap bounds, can be substituted directly into Theorem 1. This con- 20
21   nects the attained fitting objective to both original-optimum regret and the range               21

22   of the original-law cost observation. Each readout minimizes its stated one-step                 22

23   qualification bound conditional on the fixed future. It does not minimize the joint              23

24   multi-date range, the true policy loss, or a hidden-feature objective. The next back- 24
25   ward step recomputes its labels and moduli from the continuation actually returned. 25
26       The label construction in (8) uses exact conditional moments for a shallow ReLU              26

27   future and a verified continuous-action cover. Its work is charged in addition to the            27

28   linear program, coefficient rationalization, modulus calculation and dual check. A               28

29   conventional basis with the same number of coefficients receives the same labels                 29

30   and the same objective; it uses its own valid feature moduli. This is a comparison               30

31   of qualified approximations, not an assertion that different bases span the same                 31

32   functions or that a neural encoding has an intrinsic advantage.                                  32

3                                                                                                  3
       The attained continuation in Section A is an economic object, not merely an
4                                                                                                  4
     optional action proposal. Its qualification must charge the represented function
5
     rather than an avoidable featurewise overestimate. We make this comparison ex- 5
6                                                                                                  6
     plicit and then pass from an attained fit to a class of optimization outputs with the
7                                                                                                  7
     same implemented policy. All statements in this section retain the original two-date
8                                                                                                  8
     investment law, fixed terminal payoff, acquired cells and feasible action menus.
9                                                                                                  9

     For the ReLU basis, ψj (s) = (s − ( j − 1)/4)+ . For the Bernstein basis, ψj (s) =

     (4j )s j (1 − s)4− j ; the omitted zeroth coefficient on each coordinate is zero. Both repre- 16

23   These are state Lipschitz moduli for the ℓ1 distance. The first is exact for the repre-       23

     8

1        P ROPOSITION 4—Affine-exact qualification: The moduli in (11)–(12) give valid                 1

2    residual enclosures and the residual-oscillation upper bound (13). The objective is convex and    2

3    admits a finite linear-programming representation. For every common affine continuation           3

4    h( x ) = a + ux1 + vx2 , whenever its two coefficient representations are feasible, both moduli   4

5    equal max{|u|, |v|}. Thus, for common labels, node cover and target modulus, the two              5

6    qualification objectives agree on this affine function.                                           6

7                                                                                                      7
         P ROOF : A ReLU coordinate has the displayed cumulative slopes on its four affine
8                                                                                                      8
     intervals. The dual norm of ℓ1 is ℓ∞ , so their largest coordinate magnitude gives
9                                                                                                      9
     the state modulus. A Bernstein derivative is a convex combination of 4(θij − θi,j−1 )
10                                                                                                     10
     with degree-three Bernstein weights. This proves its bound. Residuals change
11                                                                                                     11
     by at most ( G + L)ρ between a node and a covered state, proving the enclosure
12                                                                                                     12
     and its oscillation bound. Each term in (13) is a maximum of finitely many affine
13                                                                                                     13
     functions. For an affine function, the ReLU coefficients are (u, 0, 0, 0) and (v, 0, 0, 0);
14                                                                                                     14
     the Bernstein coefficients are (u/4, u/2, 3u/4, u) and the analogous vector for v. All
15                                                                                                     15
     relevant slopes or derivative controls are u or v.                                     Q.E.D.
16                                                                                                     16

17       This calibration does not identify the two non-affine function classes, equalize              17

18   every numerical conditioning effect, or remove all conservatism from Bernstein                    18

19   controls. It isolates a particular qualification difference. For example, the old co- 19
20   efficientwise modulus charges ux1 as |u| in its ReLU encoding but as 10|u| in its                 20

21   Bernstein encoding. The calibrated modulus charges both as |u|. The factorial exper- 21
22   iment below retains the old fits and evaluates the new fits separately; the calibration           22

23   is not an ex post correction of their recorded costs.                                             23

17   If all cells are certified, every readout in that sublevel gives the same complete policy and the   17

18   same original-law cost. Under the uniform initial law on [0, 1]2 and an n-by-n equal-cell           18

19   cover, qϵ is exactly the fraction of uncertified cells; boundary assignments do not change          19

22                                                                                                       22
     problem and hence F (θ ) ≥ DCb ≥ DC . This contradicts F (θ ) ≤ U + ϵ < DC . Couple
23                                                                                                       23
     the attained and alternative controllers with the same initial state and innovations.
24                                                                                                       24
     On a certified initial cell their first actions agree, their next states agree, and their
25                                                                                                       25
     fixed terminal-policy actions agree. Their cash costs are identical there. Elsewhere
26                                                                                                       26
     the absolute cash-cost difference is at most H, giving (15) by integration.              Q.E.D.

28     The theorem does not replace failed switch certificates by successful ones: a non- 28
29   positive surplus is unresolved. Nor does a terminal-label certificate automatically                 29

30   propagate through newly fitted label problems at a longer horizon. The numerical                    30

31   result here concerns exactly the two-date construction for which only h1 determines                 31

32   the first-date action and the terminal actor is common.                                             32

                    C.3. Simultaneous economic conclusions for optimizer classes
14                                                                                                14
       Suppose the frozen attained-policy comparison supplies a simultaneous interval
15                                                                                                15

16
     [l jk , u jk ] for J π j − J πk . Apply Theorem 5 separately to their fixed-label sublevels, 16
     with radii v j = Hj q j and vk = Hk qk . Then, on the same event,
17                                                                                                17

18                                                                                                18
                              J πθ − J πϑ ∈ [l jk − v j − vk , u jk + v j + vk ]           (17)
19                                                                                                19

20   simultaneously for every pair of readouts in those sublevels. No union bound over            20

21   an uncountable optimizer class is needed: the extension is deterministic on the              21

22   original finite-policy event. When both covers are entirely stable the interval is           22

23   unchanged. Sharp controller-choice and deployment-allocation results in Section O            23

24   can therefore be applied directly to these qualified optimizer classes after the stated      24

25   enlargement. This transfers attained-cost evidence without identifying numeri- 25
26   cal fitting success with either population training reliability or a new economic            26

27   observation.                                                                                 27

          APPENDIX D: E CONOMIC ACCURACY OF APPROXIMATELY OPTIMIZED                               29
                                           CONTINUATIONS
30                                                                                                30

31     A qualification tolerance need not preserve every acquired action to preserve              31

32   an economic conclusion. The bound in Theorem 5 charges the full cost range on                32
                             NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                  11

1    each uncertified cell. We now retain the particular actions that remain possible and              1

2    the direction of their economic effects. This gives a cost guarantee for an entire                2

3    optimization sublevel, including approximately optimal readouts, without treating                 3

4    an inconclusive switch certificate as an observed policy failure.                                 4

5      Fix a finite first-date acquisition partition C , its initial-law masses pC , a robust          5

6    finite action menu on each cell, and a common feasible continuation policy τ after                6

7    that date. These objects are held fixed throughout the comparison. The horizon may                7

8    be any finite horizon for which the conditional costs below are enclosed. Use the                 8

9                                                            b value U = F (θb), reference
     fixed-label qualification objective F, attained readout θ,                                        9

10   action aC , and switch lower bounds DCb from Section C. For ϵ ≥ 0, define                         10

11                                                                                                     11
                             AC (ϵ) = { aC } ∪ {b ̸= aC : DCb ≤ U + ϵ}.                        (18)
12                                                                                                     12

13                                                                                                     13
     The weak inequality deliberately retains ties. Let πθ use a minimizing critic action
14                                                                                                     14
     at each cell representative and then τ. Write Qτ ( x, b) = c0 ( x, b) + β 0 P0b J1τ ( x ), and,
15                                                                                                     15

9         P ROOF : Any critic-minimizing action b ̸= aC satisfies gCb (θ ) ≤ 0, so DCb ≤              9

10   F (θ ) ≤ U + ϵ. It therefore belongs to (18). Conditioning on the initial cell expresses         10

11   the policy-cost difference as ∑C pC ∆C (bC (θ )), which is between the endpoints in              11

12   (19). The infimum of F is at most U, giving the optimization conclusion. A cell                  12

13   retaining only aC contributes zero; the signed support bounds every other contribu- 13
14   tion. Finally add and subtract the two attained costs to obtain (21). All steps after            14

15   the attained-policy event are deterministic.                                          Q.E.D.     15

16                                                                                                    16

17     The cellwise extrema relax the common-coefficient restriction and need not be                  17

18   attained by one readout. The theorem is consequently not a sharp joint characteriza- 18
19   tion of the optimizer class. Its useful feature is different: it can retain an identified        19

20   economic comparison when some actions remain uncertified and the binary Hqϵ                      20

21   allowance is too large. The original Bellman optimum and the constructive accuracy               21

22   results are unchanged. A reference expected-loss upper bound can also be enlarged                22

23   by vϵ ; the actionwise comparison itself is not a new bound obtained by replacing                23

24   the original optimum with a menu optimum.                                                        24

25     For the executed two-date economy, the conditional contrasts are computed from                 25

26   the original stage costs, transitions and fixed terminal actor. Common innovations               26

27   preserve their actual marginal laws. Centered polynomial differences retain cancella- 27
28   tion, and rectangle queries enclose every crossed acquired cell. The final innovation            28

29   is integrated analytically. Appendix AP proves whole-bin validity, deterministic                 29

30   refinement convergence under vanishing arithmetic error, and the work bound. 30
31   It also separates integration accuracy from the independent statistical interval to              31

32   which (21) is applied.                                                                           32

1         APPENDIX E: E CONOMICALLY SUFFICIENT OPTIMIZATION ACCURACY                                1

2                                                                                                   2
       The qualification objective and the economic loss have different units. A numeri-
3                                                                                                   3
     cal stopping rule should therefore be connected to the decision it supports, rather
4                                                                                                   4
     than chosen by equating their tolerances. The actionwise transfer supplies this
5                                                                                                   5
     connection for every tolerance in an interval, not only for a finite list of optimizer
6                                                                                                   6
     outputs.
7                                                                                                   7
       For construction j, fix the labels, attained value Uj , action-switch bounds and
8                                                                                                   8
     continuation policy of Theorem 6. Fix e j > 0 and enclose every action with D jCb ≤
9                                                                                                   9
     Uj + e j . Write [v−         +
                        j ( ϵ ), v j ( ϵ )] for its class-cost band. The only possible changes of
10                                                                                                  10
     this band on [0, e j ] occur at
11                                                                                                  11

12                                                                                                  12
                         K j = {0, e j } ∪ { D jCb − Uj : 0 < D jCb − Uj ≤ e j }.
13                                                                                                  13

14                                                                                                  14
     At a knot the weak switch inequality is already active. Thus the band is constant
15                                                                                                  15
     on each interval between successive knots, with the left endpoint included and the
16                                                                                                  16
     right endpoint excluded; the final endpoint is evaluated separately if it is itself a
17                                                                                                  17
     switch knot. No interpolation across a knot is permitted.
18                                                                                                  18

6                                                                                                           6

7                                                                                                           7

11                                                                                                          11

12                                                                                                          12

13                                                                                                          13

14                                                                                                          14

15                                                                                                          15

19   switch knots, where equality retains the newly possible actions. Theorem 6 gives                       19

29     A boundary of a sufficient region is not an identified failure boundary of the                       29

30   true optimizer class. The cellwise relaxation can combine actions that no common                       30

31   coefficient vector selects. Nor does a strict region necessarily include its supremum                  31

32   tolerance: at an exact switch knot, the added action can destroy the desired strict                    32

6                                                                                                  6
       An approximately optimized continuation is one function. Its coefficients deter-
7                                                                                                  7
     mine the action in every observation cell. Separate action-switch tests deliberately
8                                                                                                  8

9                                                                                                  9

10                                                                                                 10
     jointly generated by the same readout. We retain these restrictions when transfer-
11                                                                                                 11
     ring the original NBO qualification account to an economic decision. The attained
12                                                                                                 12
     continuation, economic primitives, action menu and policy-cost observations are
13                                                                                                 13
     unchanged.

      APPENDIX G: S HARED POLICY PATTERNS AND SHARP OCCUPATION BOUNDS

20     A common continuation restricts an entire contingent plan, including its pre- 20
21   scriptions at states that are not reached. Pairwise exclusions need not retain that           21

22   restriction after the occupation variables are relaxed. The following construction            22

23   preserves the complete pattern before relaxing the state distribution. It strengthens         23

24   Theorem 6, rather than replacing the original Bellman problem with an unrelated               24

25   finite-policy benchmark.                                                                      25

26     Use the original continuous-law model, reference policy, acquisition cells and              26

27   whole-cell feasible action rules of Section 5. A pattern σ assigns one menu label             27

28   σ (t, C ) to every dated cell. Let Σϵ be the patterns actually induced by members of Θϵ ,     28

29   with the stipulated tie convention. This is a finite set even when the parameter set          29

30   is infinite. For validity, it is sufficient to retain a finite superset Σϵ containing every   30

31   actual pattern and satisfying all previously certified conflicts. A numerical search          31

32   that finds some patterns does not establish such coverage. Conversely, asserting that         32
     20

1    a retained pattern is attained requires a qualified parameter witness; a fractional                       1

2    solution is not that witness.                                                                             2

8    In particular, exact finite-state transitions and advantages give the exact class-cost extrema,   8

9    attained by qualified patterns. More generally, any refinement sequence for which the dis- 9
10   played E tends to zero yields convergence of both bounds to the corresponding true extrema.       10

11   The stated transition-width condition, not refinement alone, licenses this conclusion.            11

12                                                                                                     12
       The full proof appears in Supplement 12. It does not assume that the cell process of
13                                                                                                     13
     the continuous economy is Markov. Conditional cell-to-cell probabilities exist under
14                                                                                                     14
     each fixed policy at each date; the uniform enclosures contain those probabilities
15                                                                                                     15
     regardless of the within-cell distribution. Disaggregation prevents an optimization
16                                                                                                     16
     relaxation from buying one parameter pattern initially and a different pattern after
17                                                                                                     17
     observing its induced state.
18                                                                                                     18

19                                                                                                     19
       Why complete patterns add information. The two-date example in Supplement 8
20                                                                                                     20
     has two qualified signs of one shared coefficient. One sign visits the right-hand
21                                                                                                     21
     state and then selects its free action. The other sign selects the costly action there but
22                                                                                                     22
     never visits that state. Every actual policy therefore has cost zero. The pair-conflict
23                                                                                                     23
     flow relaxation permits mass 1/2 on the right-hand state and its costly action; its
24                                                                                                     24
     upper bound is 1/2. Disaggregation by the two attainable patterns gives upper
25                                                                                                     25
     bound zero. This strict improvement requires no new policy-cost sample, changed
26                                                                                                     26
     economic primitive or successful nonconvex fit.
27                                                                                                     27

28     Computation and scope. The number of complete patterns can be exponential in                    28

29   the number of dated cells. Enumeration is therefore a finite exact implementation,                29

30   not a general polynomial-time claim. One may begin with an outer covering of                      30

31   parameter space, retain every unresolved pattern and refine only economically                     31

32   material branches. Every discarded branch needs a valid exclusion for the actual                  32
     22

1    own-future qualification functional. Missing a branch invalidates the bound; re- 1
2    taining an unresolved branch only weakens it. The state-flow width in (32) and the                                2

3    global-pattern relaxation are distinct approximation costs. This distinction preserves                            3

4    both the original continuous economy and the original-optimum policy sandwich                                     4

5    while making the added qualification work explicit.                                                               5

6                                                                                                                      6

7             APPENDIX H: O RIGINAL - LAW QUALIFICATION UNDER TRANSPORT                                                7

8                                                   APPROXIMATION                                                      8

9
       The occupation bounds retain a complete policy and its induced state distribution. 9
10                                                                                                                     10
     Their cellwise convergence result, however, requires shrinking transition-probability
11                                                                                                                     11
     widths. Moving a deterministic successor by an arbitrarily small distance need
12                                                                                                                     12
     not make those widths small. We develop a complementary certificate that tests
13                                                                                                                     13
     the transition approximation against the actual stored continuation. It applies to
14                                                                                                                     14
     singular as well as diffuse transition laws and does not require a continuous greedy
15                                                                                                                     15
     actor.
16                                                                                                                     16
       Work in the original finite-horizon expected-cost model, with compact metric
17                                                                                                                     17
     state spaces, nonempty feasible action correspondences, bounded costs and terminal
18                                                                                                                     18
     cost g. All kernels below are probability kernels with finite first moments. Minima
19                                                                                                                     19
     may be replaced by infima provided a measurable feasible approximate minimizer
20
     is supplied. Write B0 = 1 and Bt = ∏s<t β s , where β s ≥ 0. A parameter θ determines                             20

21                                                                                                                     21
     all stored continuations f t,θ and a complete feasible policy πθ . Parameters, actions
22                                                                                                                     22
     and continuations can be shared across dates and economic contexts; the state
23                                                                                                 23
     includes every endogenous variable needed by the original model.
24

25
     imation. The latter is an integration device, not a substituted economic primitive. 25

21   Thus any common-parameter qualification class for which Rθ ≤ ε is a class of complete                    21

22   policies with original-law loss at most ε. The result permits changes at every date and all              22

23   corresponding changes in occupation. It requires neither a fixed continuation after the first            23

24   date, independence of deployment contexts, nor Lipschitz continuity of the implemented                   24

25   policy.                                                                                                  25

26     The certificate is invariant to replacing each stored continuation by f t,θ + k t,θ , where the        26

27   shifts are state independent, and shifting its approximate action values consistently. If the            27

28   numerical transition is obtained by a measurable successor quantizer qt with d(y, qt (y)) ≤              28

29   ht almost surely, then et = ht is valid even when the two kernels are mutually singular. Its             29

30   additional contribution is at most                                                                       30

1    In particular, the transport part vanishes along any refinement for which this explicit            1

2    sum tends to zero. No total-variation convergence is required. Convergence of the full             2

3    certificate additionally requires the displayed residual, action-search, terminal and arithmetic   3

4    contributions to vanish.                                                                           4

5                                                                                                       5

6      The full proof is in Supplement 14. It compares the candidate and the original                   6

7    Bellman optimum separately to the same stored continuation, before subtracting                     7

8    their bounds. Applying a baseline occupation law to a different complete policy is                 8

9    neither necessary nor valid. The use of a uniform continuation modulus is essen- 9
10   tial: a small transport distance alone does not control integration of an arbitrary                10

11   discontinuous value function.                                                                      11

12                                                                                                      12

13     What the representation contributes. For the constructed affine–ReLU continua- 13
14   tions, a valid Lipschitz modulus follows either from the cone construction or from                 14

15   verified layer norms. The resulting Kt+1 prices transition approximation in the                    15

16   policy certificate. The exact same function represented by a native minimum circuit                16

17   has the same mathematical guarantee. The result identifies the role of the neural                  17

18   continuation without making a method-neutral verifier evidence of universal neural                 18

19   speed. Wasserstein robustness and finite-model approximation have established                      19

20   analyses (Zhou et al., 2024, Saldi et al., 2015); our use is the uniform, translation- 20
21   invariant qualification account for shared NBO continuations and their complete                    21

22   original-law policies.                                                                             22

8    When fixed, verified continuation moduli and primitive resource estimates yield                8

9    a positive-monomial upper model in (η, e), Theorem 19 applies to its positive- 9
10   weight coordinates. Coordinates with zero budget weight require separate caps and              10

11   treatment; they do not satisfy that theorem’s positive-weight premise. A modulus               11

13   upper certificate. This preserves the cost of transition precision rather than hiding it       13

14   in the number of acquired states.                                                              14

15                                                                                                  15

16                           H.2. Nonlinear and strategic evaluations                               16

17
       For a cash-invariant monotone one-step risk evaluation, the same signed recur- 17
18                                                                                                  18
     sion applies when a separately established bound on the risk-evaluation discrepancy
19                                                                                                  19
     replaces bt . Wasserstein distance and cash invariance alone do not imply such a
20                                                                                                  20
     bound. General monotone Lipschitz aggregators use the absolute-error recursion
21                                                                                                  21
     of Proposition 7, with their own continuation moduli, rather than an unsupported
22
     linear expectation identity. For games, the transport account enters each fixed- 22
23                                                                                                  23
     opponent best-response certificate separately. Preference states and temporal selves
24                                                                                                  24
     retain the augmented-state and unilateral-deviation interpretation already stated in
25                                                                                                  25
     Section 5.1. The new result supplements, rather than removes, the original diffusion,
26                                                                                                  26
     recursive-preference and game constructions.
27                                                                                                  27

28     A singular-transition diagnostic. Take one decision date, β = 1, terminal cost               28

29   g( x ) = x on [0, 1], and actions 0 and 1. Action 0 has zero immediate cost and true           29

30   successor h > 0; action 1 costs h/2 and has successor zero. The numerical kernel               30

31   places both successors at zero. With f 0 = 0 and f 1 = g, its greedy choice is action 0, its   31

32   residual span is zero and its true regret is h/2. Omitting the transition charge would         32
     26

1    falsely certify zero regret. The transport certificate is 2h and converges to zero, while            1

2    the total-variation distance for action 0 is one for every h > 0. Appendix AW gives                  2

3    exact rational checks. This diagnostic is not a new observation in the investment                    3

4    experiment.                                                                                          4

6               APPENDIX I: S EQUENTIAL QUALIFICATION OF POLICY COSTS                                     6

7      A fixed-sample interval does not by itself justify repeated inspection until a                     7

8    controller appears acceptable. Nor do cost observations from changing controllers                    8

9    identify the last controller’s cost. We extend the frozen-continuation identity to                   9

10   predictable episodes and distinguish these targets. This uses established time- 10
11   uniform concentration principles (Howard et al., 2020); confidence sequences have                    11

12   also been used for gated deployment in contextual bandits (Karampatziakis et al.,                    12

13   2021). Here the observations are verified, continuous-law NBO cost contrasts, not                    13

14   importance-weighted bandit rewards.                                                                  14

31   Each endpoint may also be intersected with the corresponding average predictable support             31

32   endpoint. The assertion holds at every almost surely finite stopping time. If the complete           32
                               NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                    27

1    controller pair and initial-law/reset contract stay fixed, then ∆i = ∆, even when the qualified       1

2    continuation changes predictably between episodes. Otherwise the target is the average                2

3    conditional cost difference, not the last controller’s cost.                                          3

4                                                                                                          4
       The proof applies conditional Hoeffding bounds to each endpoint and Ville’s
5                                                                                                          5
     inequality to a finite collection of nonnegative supermartingales. It requires no
6                                                                                                          6
     independent or identically distributed deployment contexts. It does require the
7                                                                                                          7
     stated conditional transition laws. Within-episode cache adaptation is allowed only
8                                                                                                          8
     when the controllers are treated as history-dependent algorithms and the greedy-
9                                                                                                          9
     gap qualification covers every memory state that can occur. Repeating the same
10                                                                                                         10
     program identifier while carrying a different cache state does not establish a fixed
11                                                                                                         11
     conditional target.
12                                                                                                         12
       The implementation uses a finite dyadic collection of λ j and exact rational upper
13                                                                                                         13
     bounds for the logarithms. These give conservative, computable versions of (38).
14                                                                                                         14
     It reserves the support and target identity before accepting an episode. A failed
15                                                                                                         15
     numerical evaluation consumes the full previously qualified support interval and
16                                                                                                         16
     its actual work; it is not deleted after inspecting its outcome. A failure of the support
17                                                                                                         17
     certificate itself stops qualification.
18                                                                                                         18

19     C OROLLARY 13—Costed replacement after optional stopping: For a fixed target                        19

20   pair, let [l N , u N ] be the interval at a finite stopping time N. Let a declared deployment count   20

21   be m ≥ 1, a one-time replacement and qualification charge be F ≥ 0, and the incremental               21

22   per-deployment charge be c, all in the same units as the policy cost. On the simultaneous             22

23   event of Theorem 12, the net incremental cost lies in                                                 23

24                                                                                                         24

25                                 [l N + F/m + c, u N + F/m + c].                                         25

26                                                                                                         26
     Consequently u N + F/m + c < 0 certifies strict net improvement. Inclusion of [l N , u N ] in
27                                                                                                         27
     a declared equivalence band certifies equivalence at that band, not strict superiority. New
28                                                                                                         28
     adaptively selected controller pairs may be qualified in separate epochs with conditional
29                                                                                                         29
     error allocations whose sum is at most the overall error budget.
30                                                                                                         30

31     A wall-clock reading is not a monetary charge. Measured setup, validation,                          31

32   rejected evaluations and successful retrievals are retained separately; prices are                    32
     28

1    explicit decision inputs. The number of qualification episodes is not automatically          1

2    the number of deployments over which a setup cost is amortized. These distinctions           2

3    let the original investment decision use a valid stopping rule without silently              3

4    changing either its estimand or its units.                                                   4

6         APPENDIX J: O RIGINAL - LAW QUALIFICATION OF CONSTRUCTED POLICIES                       6

7                                                                                                 7
       A continuation can have a verified fitting objective without its implemented
8                                                                                                 8
     policy having an identified economic advantage. The distinction is resolved by
9                                                                                                 9
     evaluating the same complete policy that the construction returns. We now connect
10                                                                                                10
     the qualification-optimal readouts of Section B to independent original-law cost
11                                                                                                11
     observations. Neither the fitted future nor the acquired actor is replaced during
12                                                                                                12
     evaluation. The comparison concerns the constructed NBO policies, rather than a
13                                                                                                13
     different recursive controller supplied with the same neural label.
14                                                                                                14

15                                                                                                15

     A paired difference has the signed support [− H, H ]. Negative common-path gains
9                                                                                               9
     are not clipped. No original-optimum value query is required to compute either
10                                                                                              10
     observation for a frozen acquired actor.
11                                                                                              11
       An initial or innovation bin is a continuous cell, not a point mass. The evalua-
12                                                                                              12
     tor encloses every true state in the bin, every acquired cell reached by that state
13                                                                                              13
     enclosure, and every resulting feasible action. When an enclosure crosses an acqui-
14                                                                                              14
     sition boundary, all intersected actions enter the continuation enclosure. A second
15                                                                                              15
     implementation evaluates the same primitives using integer-directed arithmetic
16                                                                                              16
     and expanded polynomials. The reported endpoints are the hull of the two valid
17                                                                                              17
     enclosures, intersected with the proved support. Arithmetic agreement is a check
18                                                                                              18
     on the implementations; validity follows from the enclosure rules and the primitive
19                                                                                              19
     identities, not from agreement alone.

22                            J.2. Precision and the decision criterion                         22

23                                                                                              23
       The following statement applies to any fixed finite collection of qualified policies,
24                                                                                              24
     not only to the two feature bases used below. All construction information is fixed
25                                                                                              25
     before the independent evaluation rows are drawn. Dependence among policies on
26                                                                                              26
     a common row is allowed.

15                                                                                                    15

18                                                                                                    18

19     The proof in the Technical Supplement applies the empirical-Bernstein inequality               19

20   of Maurer and Pontil (2009) to the lower and upper endpoint variables. The statis- 20
21   tical inequality is established work. The contribution here is its application to the            21

22   original-law costs of the actual constructed actors, with executable whole-bin obser- 22
23   vations, independent arithmetic and an explicit precision account. Refining only a               23

24   simulation bin cannot remove the finite-support or sampling terms. Conversely, an                24

25   inaccurate uniform regret certificate need not prevent an accurate comparison of                 25

26   two actual policy costs.                                                                         26

27     Precision, equivalence and improvement are different conclusions. For a cost- 27
28   difference interval [l, u] and a predeclared margin δ, equivalence requires −δ < l ≤             28

29   u < δ. Noninferiority of the first policy requires u < δ; strict superiority requires            29

30   u < 0. An interval can meet its width target and fail equivalence because it identifies          30

31   a material difference. These decisions are made from exact endpoints, not from                   31

32   rounded table entries.                                                                           32
                              NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                    31

1      C OROLLARY 15—Priced selection and adoption: On the simultaneous event of                          1

2    Theorem 14, let [ L j , Uj ] qualify the cost of policy j, and let w j be its declared nonnegative   2

3    resource charge. A minimizer bj of Uj + w j satisfies, for every policy j,                           3

4                                                                                                         4
                                     Jbj + wbj ≤ Jj + w j + (Uj − L j ).
5                                                                                                         5

6                                                                                                         6
     The inequality holds for every assignment of resource prices on the same event. If the gain
7                                                                                                         7
     from replacing an installed controller is in [l, u], then m deployments, monetary scale v ≥ 0,
8                                                                                                         8
     qualification charge q ≥ 0 and replacement fee f ≥ 0 give expected net benefit in
9                                                                                                         9

10                                   [mvl − q − f , mvu − q − f ].                                        10

11                                                                                                        11
     In particular, when l > 0 and v > 0, every m > (q + f )/(vl ) is certified beneficial.
12                                                                                                        12

13     P ROOF : The first assertion follows from Jbj ≤ Ubj , the minimizing property, and                 13

14   Uj ≤ Jj + Uj − L j . These deterministic inequalities hold for all nonnegative prices                14

15   once all cost intervals cover. The second assertion is multiplication by mv and                      15

16   subtraction of q + f .                                                                    Q.E.D.     16

17                                                                                                        17

18
       The resource charges in this result must be specified, not inferred from normalized                18

19
     losses. An already paid construction and a prospective purchase have different                       19

20
     charges. When only a shared qualification service has been timed, charging that                      20

21
     entire service to a proposed replacement is conservative; inventing a per-policy                     21

22
     marginal qualification time is not.                                                                  22

23                                                                                                        23
                              J.3. The constructed-controller experiment
24                                                                                                        24

25     The fixed catalogue consists of the four previously constructed NBO actors and                     25

26   the admissible zero-investment incumbent. ReLU and degree-four Bernstein con- 26
27   tinuations each have nine coefficients and are constructed on eight- or sixteen-cell                 27

28   coordinate covers. At a fixed cover, the two bases use the same acquisition con- 28
29   tract and primitive information. The actor arrays, actual fitted futures and exact                   29

30   optimization certificates are unchanged. The zero-investment policy supplies an                      30

31   economically interpretable installed reference; it is not substituted for the strong                 31

32   conventional query competitors in the preceding study.                                               32
     32

1      Before execution, the protocol fixes N = 524,288, forty-bit continuous bins, the                          1

2    sample stream, the full-width target 1/256, and the separate two-sided equivalence                          2

3    margin 1/256. All five marginal costs and ten paired differences are reported                               3

4    for both observations in (40)–(41). Thus K = 30 and ℓ = 10 give a joint failure                             4

5    bound below 1/100. Reusing a row for another controller or estimator does not                               5

6    increase the independent sample count. The declared independence model concerns                             6

7    the simulated rows; a fixed pseudorandom seed establishes reproducibility, not                              7

8    mathematical independence.                                                                                  8

1      Outcomes of the fixed precision design. Across both estimators, 20 of the twenty         1

2    paired intervals meet the declared full-width target. Their widths range from              2

3    0.000936 to 0.002285. Exactly 4 meet the separate equivalence criterion. Neither a         3

4    failed width target nor a failed equivalence comparison is omitted.                        4

5      The conditionally integrated comparisons identify a positive gain over the zero- 5
6    investment incumbent for 4 of the four constructed policies. These are gains of            6

7    complete implemented controllers, not reductions in a qualification objective.             7

8      At the two matched acquisition resolutions, the ReLU-minus-Bernstein compar- 8
9    isons identify 2 lower-cost ReLU outcomes, 0 higher-cost outcomes, and 0 unre- 9
10   solved signs. This finite comparison is not a universal representation ranking.            10

11     The construction and the evaluation now refer to the same complete controllers. 11
12   The resulting cost intervals, rather than a reduction in the residual-fitting objective,   12

13   determine the adoption regions in Corollary 15. Comparisons between the feature            13

14   bases identify only what their paired intervals support. The earlier unresolved and        14

15   adverse query-method comparisons remain distinct evidence and are preserved in             15

16   the Numerical and Development Appendix.                                                    16

17     Both interval implementations evaluate every new trajectory and every acquired- 17
18   action rectangle. Additional rational midpoint checks test predetermined rows. The         18

19   phase ledger separates controller loading, lookup preparation, binary64 evaluation,        19

20   integer evaluation, exact statistical arithmetic, output and the complete parent           20

21   process. The independent replay recomputes the entire new enclosure catalogue              21

22   without replacing its original clocks. This evaluates the two-date constructed-policy      22

23   problem; it is not an independent rerun of the older, much larger vector Bellman           23

24   tree.                                                                                      24

26                                                                                              26
     APPENDIX K: Q UALIFICATION , REPRESENTATION , AND ATTAINED ECONOMIC
27                                                                                              27
                                              COST
28                                                                                              28

29     The preceding attained-controller experiment used a coefficientwise qualifica- 29
30   tion bound. Its ReLU–Bernstein ordering is an observation about those specified            30

31   constructions. We now examine whether that ordering persists after the two bases           31

32   receive the same qualification charge on their common affine functions. This directly      32
     34

1    tests a source of representation-dependent conservatism while preserving the origi- 1
2    nal nonlinear investment economy and the Neural Bellman Operator construction. 2
3                                                                                             3

4                                                                                             4
                            K.1. A prospective factorial comparison
5                                                                                             5

6      The design fixes two bases, two qualification rules and two acquisition resolutions. 6
7    The eight constructed controllers and the installed zero controller are evaluated on     7

8    the same original-law paths. Each basis has nine coefficients with absolute bounds       8

9    eight, the same node covers, thirty-three-action search menus and downward action        9

10   quantum 1/4096. The calibrated rule replaces the coefficientwise modulus by (11)         10

11   or (12), both in residual qualification and in the regularity allowance passed to the    11

12   previous fitted date. Both own-future continuations are constructed backwards. An        12

13   exact primal–dual gap of at most 1/65536 qualifies each new fit. The coefficient         13

14   proposal is quantized at forty bits; no hidden-feature optimizer or outcome-selected     14

15   basis is introduced.                                                                     15

16     A source manifest was committed before construction, and the complete ac- 16
17   tor identities were committed before generating 524,288 new four-coordinate              17

18   continuous-bin rows. The bin precision is forty bits. Two estimators, cash cost          18

19   and exact last-innovation conditional expectation, evaluate the same expected costs      19

20   under the original uniform initial law and continuous innovations. For each esti- 20
21   mator the frozen family contains nine marginal costs, thirty-six paired differences      21

22   and two basis-by-qualification interactions. All ninety-four estimands share a single    22

23   failure allowance bounded by 376 exp(−11) < 1/100. Shared paths and the two              23

24   estimators do not multiply the number of independent rows.                               24

25     The whole-bin enclosures are independently computed in outward binary64                25

26   arithmetic and directed-integer arithmetic. Their hull, intersected with the proved      26

27   support, is used for inference. An exact rational implementation additionally checks     27

28   512 midpoint trajectories per controller. All nine controllers, bins, endpoints, exact   28

29   sample moments and disagreements are part of the retained protocol. This revision        29

30   re-evaluates every bin and every inference quantity; it neither regenerates those        30

31   observations as a second sample nor substitutes replay time for the original service     31

32   clock.                                                                                   32

                                K.2. Precision of the economic comparison
19                                                                                                          19

20     The target was a paired total width of 1/256, fixed before seeing the new rows. 20
21   Appendix AO reports every interval and its width components, including every                           21

22   marginal interval whether or not its width falls below that paired target. Vari- 22
23   ance reduction by conditional integration affects the variance component, not the                      23

24   bounded-support penalty or the acquisition contract. For example, in the fine cali- 24
25   brated basis comparison the conditional interval’s width consists of approximately                     25

26   0.001008605 from the finite-support term, 0.000048152 from endpoint sampling- 26
27   variance allowances, and 2.69 × 10−11 from the numerical enclosure. The support                        27

28   term is therefore the principal remaining contribution in that comparison.                             28

29     The numerical component includes the implemented handling of continuous-bin                          29

30   width, acquisition ambiguity and arithmetic propagation. It is not represented as                      30

31   a pure floating-point error estimate. Likewise, a small numerical component does                       31

32   not justify dropping the finite-sample support term. These diagnostics explain the                     32
                                NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                          37
