22                                                                                                          22
                              AB.1. Complete timing and retained records
23                                                                                                          23

24     The process receipt includes creation, Python imports, original Bellman fills, all                   24

25   recursive descendants, cache retrieval, rational checks, repeated serialization, final                 25

26   output and process exit. The driver writes a separate receipt after that boundary. 26
27   Summing child block clocks is not the complete process time. Every block’s body                        27

28   prefix excludes imports and has its own exact definition in the source. The complete                   28

29   receipts, rather than those prefixes, determine the reported timing comparison.                        29

30     No processor frequency lock is claimed. The start and end records include the                        30

31   selected CPU, frequency fields where the environment exposes them, interpreter                         31

32   version and platform. A cyclic mode order limits a simple fixed-order confound                         32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                            83

1    but does not create randomized hardware-frequency inference. Close clock differ- 1
2    ences require more evidence than the prescribed finite repetitions. Bellman action        2

3    counts are consequently reported separately from seconds. They are invariant de- 3
4    scriptive counts for these transcripts, not predictions of another implementation’s       4

5    throughput.                                                                               5

6      The 128-record cap bounds logical retained witnesses. It does not bound total           6

7    process resident memory: a fresh recursive fill can retain batched descendants,           7

8    interpreter state, interval work arrays and output buffers. The largest process           8

9    memory remains in the table. The executed cache operates at workload roots only. 9
10   The four-date pressure is not hidden by counting a later root hit as though the first     10

11   fill used no descendants.                                                                 11

12                                                                                             12

13                                                                                             13
                         AB.2. Independent checks and trusted premises
14                                                                                             14

15     The additional admission audit matches all 708 returns to the prescribed state,         15

16   control dimension, horizon, tolerance and context epoch. Cache reloads in this            16

17   catalogue use the same process and numerical model. Arbitrarily supplied external         17

18   intervals are not made valid by deserialization or a file hash.                           18

19     For a returned hit, the independent checker locates the earlier paid source record      19

20   with the same context, horizon, source state, action and endpoints. It recomputes         20

21   the original regularity constants, state distance, action displacement and interval       21

22   expansion in exact rational arithmetic. It checks the returned action’s lattice and       22

23   capacity and that the serialized binary endpoints contain the exact expanded in- 23
24   terval. A narrow stored interval alone would not prove that a source fill encloses        24

25   the original value: the inherited verifier and its primitive proof remain explicit        25

26   premises.                                                                                 26

27     For conditional ReLU integration, the second implementation reconstructs the            27

28   original projected drift and shocks independently. It does not call the divided-power     28

29   formula. It clips the support rectangle against the affine neuron’s positive half-plane   29

30   and uses oriented area and first moments. Degenerate projections reduce to one- 30
31   dimensional integration or a deterministic positive part. All rational discrepancies      31

32   are fatal to the new arithmetic audit and are retained as evidence if encountered.        32
     84

1      The earlier R70 independent cash-path audit and final inference arithmetic are        1

2    unchanged. The historical full descendant trees are not certified by pretending that    2

3    a file hash is a mathematical proof. Neither the new rational transfer checks nor the   3

4    polygon integrations independently regenerate all those trees. The release states       4

5    this boundary in its machine-readable audit and response.                               5

6                                                                                            6

7                   AB.3. Development probe and publication preservation                     7

8                                                                                            8
       Before the new source commitment, a vector development probe queried (0.4, 0.6)
9                                                                                            9
     at tolerance 0.04 and horizons two, three and four. The first two returned. The probe
10                                                                                           10
     was stopped at its 35-second process limit while computing horizon four. Its log is
11                                                                                           11
     retained separately and is not merged with the later 900-second scientific catalogue
12                                                                                           12
     or used as a favorable timing observation.
13                                                                                           13
       Every R70 active section is assigned to exactly one current document and checked
14                                                                                           14
     byte for byte. All inherited formal statements and proofs are thereby retained,
15                                                                                           15
     not reconstructed from a favorable summary. The complete old main article and
16                                                                                           16
     supplement remain companion PDFs. New tables are derived from the committed
17                                                                                           17
     receipts and independent audit, and all four current documents are rebuilt from a
18                                                                                           18
     clean source directory. Visual inspection is documented against the exact PDF hashes
19                                                                                           19
     actually inspected. Compilation and tests do not constitute external mathematical
20                                                                                           20
     acceptance.

22                                                                                           22
          APPENDIX AC: C OMPLETE CONTINUATION - QUALIFICATION RECORDS
23                                                                                           23

24     The fixed design was committed before any new construction service ran. Its four      24

25   points are the product of covers with 82 or 162 state nodes and two nine-coefficient    25

26   bases. The terminal continuation is the original g; each preceding date is fitted       26

27   against the continuation actually returned at the following date. The stage label is    27

28   an interval containing the full continuous-action Bellman infimum. The coefficient      28

29   box, quantum, feature knots, action-cover size and optimization tolerance were          29

30   fixed in advance.                                                                       30

31     The ReLU basis consists of the constant and, in each coordinate, the identity and     31

32   positive parts at 1/4, 1/2 and 3/4. The conventional basis consists of the constant     32

       LS is bounded least squares on the identical features and exact fixed-future interval midpoints. Its
13                                                                                                              13

14    controller. The primal and dual calculations are exact rational checks, independently reconstructed       14

15                                  without relying on the optimizer status.                                    15

16                                                                                                              16

17   and the four nonconstant degree-four Bernstein polynomials per coordinate. Their                           17

18   valid feature moduli are respectively one and four. Having the same number of                              18

19   coefficients does not make their function classes or approximation moduli identical. 19
20   Each basis receives its own actual fixed-future labels and the same construction                           20

21   rule; the label-loss ablation within each stage shares those exact labels and features. 21
22     Every linear-program output is rounded into its declared coefficient box and                             22

23   checked with rational probability multipliers. The table’s gap is the achieved pri- 23
24   mal value minus the exact dual lower bound, not the solver’s reported duality                              24

25   tolerance. Constant recentering changes the continuation gauge without changing                            25

26   this objective. The predeclared success tolerance is 1/65536. The stored status and                        26

27   message remain even when the exact certificate succeeds; an unsuccessful optimizer                         27

28   would retain its feasible fallback and its actual gap rather than disappear from the                       28

29   catalogue.                                                                                                 29

31   feature-mean evaluations, the qualification optimizer, its rationalization, the                            31

32   matched bounded least-squares ablation and the service output. The separate clock                          32

15   record identifies its endpoint. These are single recorded construction times; no esti- 15
16   mate of mean runtime, clock stability or a universal neural speed ratio is inferred. 16
17   The independent audit has its own time and never replaces a scientific service time. 17
18   Exact arithmetic-operation counts are not a bound on rational bit complexity.                              18

19     The independent action-value evaluator does not invoke the production positive- 19
20   part moment expansion. It partitions the innovation interval at every ReLU break- 20
21   point and integrates affine pieces. For the degree-four conventional continuation                          21

22   it uses the positive Boole weights on five equally spaced points; the result is exact                      22

23   for this polynomial degree. Final-date values are checked against the retained origi- 23
24   nal rational investment primitive. It also reconstructs every feasible node action,                        24

25   the original continuous-action lower enclosure, the robust acquired action and                             25

26   its whole-cell gap. Thus the newly computed residual account is tied to original                           26

27   primitives rather than only to a stored hash.                                                              27

29   ence. The support supplied by its fitted continuation is available to Theorem 12,                          29

31   fixed-sample comparisons, two-control workloads and cache processes retain their                           31

32   original observations, contracts and coverage accounts.                                                    32

1              APPENDIX AD: E XACT SEQUENTIAL - LEDGER REGRESSIONS                             1

2                                                                                              2
       The new regression suite checks conditional means, range reservation, target
3                                                                                              3
     changes and failed numerical episodes. Its adaptive binary-tree diagnostic enumer-
4                                                                                              4
     ates all 212 = 4096 leaves and every prefix, with exact probabilities and changing
5                                                                                              5
     predictable ranges. The event count is a finite-model check of the implemented
6                                                                                              6
     boundary, not an empirical substitute for the supermartingale proof. Rational loga-
7                                                                                              7
     rithm bounds and coefficient duals are also tested independently of floating solver
8                                                                                              8
     stopping flags.
9                                                                                              9
       A support failure and a numerical failure are deliberately different branches.
10                                                                                             10
     An empty intersection or a changed fixed target is rejected. A numerical failure
11                                                                                             11
     records the prequalified full support, advances the prefix and charges its work. The
12                                                                                             12
     interface cannot prove that a caller fixed the support before observing the episode;
13                                                                                             13
     that temporal and probabilistic premise remains part of the algorithm’s contract. A
14                                                                                             14
     cache carried across episodes is included in the target definition or is handled as
15                                                                                             15
     varying conditional cost.
16                                                                                             16
       All prior scientific arrays and sources remain unchanged. The ordinary-source
17                                                                                             17
     build runs the inherited and current tests and recomputes the published tables.
18                                                                                             18
     The clean archive includes the transitive R62 input sources, including the original
19                                                                                             19
     service kernel omitted by the interrupted R72 archive. The reproduction report
20                                                                                             20
     identifies the suites run inside that archive and compares actual PDF text and
21                                                                                             21
     page counts after deleting generated PDFs and tables. Visual inspection is recorded
22                                                                                             22
     separately against the output hashes. None of those publication checks is an external
23                                                                                             23
     mathematical acceptance decision.

26                                                                                             26
           APPENDIX AE: T RANSPORTING PURCHASED B ELLMAN WITNESSES
27                                                                                             27

28     A second way to control the descendant cost is to reuse a calculation already           28

29   purchased. Exact memoization only recognizes the same state. The following rule           29

30   transfers a lower Bellman bound and its feasible upper witness to a nearby state,         30

31   after repairing capacity and accounting for the full displacement. It is compatible       31

32   with either a neural or a conventional generator of the original certified record.        32
     88

27   Hence outward endpoints with U ( x ) − L( x ) ≤ δ permit the actual repaired action to be        27

28   returned without a new Bellman query. An identity mismatch, failed feasibility check or          28

29   excessive width requires a new verified calculation; proximity alone licenses no reuse.          29

2    returned by a fresh search at the same tolerance.                                                 2

       The implemented repair first rounds the new capacity downward on the 2−32
5                                                                                                      5
     lattice, scales a vector action proportionally when necessary, and rounds each
6                                                                                                      6

7                                                                                                      7

8                                                                                                      8

                           AE.2. A finite-cover account and its premises

       A small number of hits does not establish a uniform reuse complexity bound.
13                                                                                                     13

14                                                                                                     14
     the action quantum be q, let m ∈ {1, 2}, and suppose every fresh record is purchased
15                                                                                                     15
     with gap at most γδ, where 0 < γ < 1. The repair just described obeys

1    The lookup and check costs include rejected candidates. Naive retrieval uses O( Mr d) arith- 1

       This bound makes the dimensional price of uniform reuse explicit. It is not a
7                                                                                                   7

          APPENDIX AF: C ONTINUATION EVALUATION AND PAID QUERY REUSE
15                                                                                                  15

16
       The evidence separates three questions: whether a certified readout returns an               16

17
     admissible policy, whether its actual expected cost meets a declared margin, and               17

18
     whether a purchased calculation can be reused profitably. The preceding training               18

19
     and inference catalogue remains unchanged. The new execution fixes localized                   19

20
     query workloads and exact conditional-moment checks before running them. It                    20

21
     changes neither the economic primitives nor the continuous feasible-action target.             21

22                                                                                                  22

23
                      AF.1. Retained trained policies and economic precision                        23

24     The retained 188-process catalogue is reproduced in Appendix, Section U. Hat– 24
25   ReLU and Bernstein readouts purchase the same teacher information and have                     25

26   exactly 5d coefficients; the achieved objective and box dual gaps are recomputed               26

27   rationally. Independent validation and sixty-four-block reuse receive separate work            27

28   accounts. Both their favorable and unfavorable repetitions remain visible. In partic- 28
29   ular, the eight-state task does not identify a learned complete-work winner.                   29

30     The direct policy-cost experiment fixes its sample count and numerical pre- 30
31   cision before execution. All twelve original-law paired intervals lie within the               31

32   declared equivalence region [−1/256, 1/256], and all contain zero. The two                     32
                            NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                               91

1    adaptive-minus-hat–ReLU intervals are approximately [−0.00068263, 0.00070764]                 1

2    and [−0.00160291, 0.00160253]. Their shared inference services cost about 9.03 and            2

3    408.63 seconds. These are informative quality comparisons with expensive qualifi- 3
4    cation, not an identified neural cost ranking or a free inference improvement. The            4

5    complete precision and adoption tables are retained in the appendix.                          5

6      The frozen-continuation theorem supplies a different way to evaluate that                   6

7    expected-cost estimand. Its moment component is executed here; a new trained                  7

8    critic meeting the old equivalence margin at lower complete qualification cost is             8

9    not presumed. The earlier optimal-advantage observations are not counted again                9

10   as samples of the new formula.                                                                10

11                                                                                                 11

12                                                                                                 12
                            AF.2. A fixed original-law query catalogue
13                                                                                                 13

14     The new design has five tasks: two scalar-control tasks at (d, T ) = (2, 2), (8, 3)         14

15   and three two-control tasks at (d, T ) = (2, 2), (2, 3), (2, 4). Each task has three rules:   15

16   fresh calculation, exact-key reuse and geometric witness transport. The source                16

17   commitment fixes the task list, tolerances, repetition counts, 128-record capacity,           17

18   invalidation schedule, workload order and 900-second per-process limit. There are             18

19   36 prescribed complete processes. None trains a new action predictor or loads a               19

20   precomputed conventional policy.                                                              20

21     A workload starts at alternating coordinates 0.4, 0.6. Odd requests repeat that             21

22   state. Predetermined even requests perturb one coordinate by 2−20 . Except in the             22

23   four-date stress case, a late distant request has every coordinate equal to 0.75, and a       23

24   specified earlier block invalidates the context. The first four tasks have 32, 16, 24         24

25   and 8 requests; the four-date task has four. The first three tasks have three timing          25

26   repetitions, the three-date vector task two, and the four-date task one. Rules are            26

27   executed in a fixed cyclic order across repetitions.                                          27

28     Every miss calls the unchanged original recursive verifier at the declared toler- 28
29   ance. Every hit uses the exact state-transfer and capacity-repair test. The cache is          29

30   serialized, hashed, reloaded and validated after every block. The receipt begins              30

31   before process creation and ends after exit, including imports, all descendant work,          31

32   file output and shutdown; writing the parent receipt itself is separate publication           32
     92

1    overhead. A single CPU is pinned. Available start and end frequency information is                        1

2    recorded, but frequency is not locked. Repetitions support descriptive comparisons,                       2

3    not a hardware-uniform or population timing theorem.                                                      3

                     AF.3. Independent conditional-moment and transport checks                                     27

28     The rational integration catalogue uses four (d, m) specifications, namely                                  28

29   (2, 1), (8, 1), (2, 2), (8, 2), and 64 state–action contexts per specification. Each context                  29

30   has sixteen rational neurons. The original bilinear state drift, action exposure and                          30

31   uniform innovation projections are evaluated exactly. An independent checker                                  31

32   clips the corresponding innovation rectangle and integrates the affine neuron                                 32
     94

1    through polygon area and first moments. It imports no production moment, cache                           1

4                    AF.4. Constructed continuations and attained qualification                            4

5      The readout theorem is executed in four predeclared backward constructions in                       5

6    the original two-state, two-date, scalar-control economy. Two midpoint covers, with                   6

7    eight or sixteen cells per coordinate, are crossed with additive shallow ReLU and                     7

8    degree-four Bernstein features. Each basis has nine coefficients bounded by eight. 8
9    At each date the future is the continuation actually constructed at the next date. 9
10   Exact conditional moments supply thirty-three continuous feasible action values per                   10

11   node and thirty-three robust, downward-quantized acquired-action proposals. The                       11

12   original state-dependent capacity and continuous innovation law are unchanged.                        12

17                                                                                                       17
                        AG.1. Acquisition, bin integration and arithmetic
18                                                                                                       18

19     Each path row contains two initial-state and two innovation bin indices. A forty- 19
20   bit bin represents its full original-law continuous cell. The implementation does not               20

21   replace the conditional distribution within the bin by a midpoint. At each date, a                  21

22   rectangle enclosing the true state can intersect several acquired cells. The selected               22

23   action range then includes every cell in that rectangle. Closed upper boundaries                    23

24   are included conservatively. The endpoint calculation propagates that entire range                  24

25   through the stage cost and next transition.                                                         25

26     A zero action and every stored constructed action are feasible on the full cell. In               26

27   particular, the action quantum is checked against the minimum state-dependent                       27

28   capacity on the cell before costs are evaluated. State uncertainty is therefore not                 28

29   silently removed by using the lower-cell center as the true state. Acquisition am- 29
30   biguity, continuous-bin variation and arithmetic expansion all contribute to the                    30

31   retained numerical width. They can interact, so a decomposition into three sepa- 31
32   rately additive causal effects is not inferred from a single interval run. The exact                32
                                NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                         99

1    aggregate numerical term is reported alongside independent ambiguity counts and                             1

2    the two arithmetic implementations’ endpoint differences.                                                   2

27   Unrounded components sum exactly to the recorded width. The numerical term is the mean retained full- 27
28   bin endpoint width, including acquisition and arithmetic effects; these are not asserted to be separately   28

29   additive causes. Variance terms use exact endpoint sample variances and upward rational square roots.       29

30     The first evaluator uses centered polynomial expressions with binary64 outward                            30

31   operations. The second expands the cost and transition polynomials and uses inte- 31
32   ger floor and ceiling on a 2−48 grid. It obtains acquired-action extrema by directly                        32
     100

1    enumerating each distinct intersected rectangle rather than reading the first evalua- 1
2    tor’s range tables. Thus it shares neither floating arithmetic nor cost, transition or   2

3    lookup routines with the production evaluator. The stored endpoints use the hull,        3

4    not the more optimistic intersection, of these two evaluations. Intersection with the    4

5    global primitive support is justified separately.                                        5

6      Every new full-bin path is recomputed by both implementations during replay. 6
7    A further exact-rational midpoint calculation checks 512 predetermined rows for          7

8    each policy, including both estimators. Synthetic boundary tests exercise bins at the    8

9    state and innovation extremes and across acquired-cell boundaries. These point- 9
10   wise tests supplement the interval-propagation proof; they are not called full-bin       10

11   verification by themselves. The historical aliasing counterexample and its corrected     11

12   R67 execution remain unchanged.                                                          12

13                                                                                            13

14                                                                                            14

15                                                                                            15

16                                                                                            16

17
                                  AG.2. Statistical allocation                                17

18                                                                                            18

19     There are 30 estimands and 60 one-sided endpoint events in one fixed family. The       19

20   empirical-Bernstein radii use the common log allowance ten. The union bound is at        20

21   most 120e−10 < 1/100, verified independently by a rational lower partial sum for         21

22   e10 . All endpoint sums and sums of squares are integer calculations, so no floating     22

23   sample-variance cancellation enters the confidence endpoints. Upward rational            23

24   square roots enlarge rather than shrink the radii.                                       24

25     The new family is distinct from the earlier R69 optimal-advantage-centered             25

26   inference family. Combining conclusions from different families requires adding          26

27   their error allowances. The R73 construction records contain no new independent          27

28   cost rows, and replay of the R77 rows adds none. The sample stream, row count,           28

29   bin precision, policy hashes, estimands and margin are fixed before execution. A         29

30   failure of the declared width target is retained; it does not authorize more samples     30

31   after inspecting the signs. No optional-stopping interpretation is assigned to this      31

32   fixed-sample experiment.                                                                 32

                                      AG.3. Paid work and adoption
15                                                                                                           15

16     The parent process. The complete five-controller service took 105.049 seconds                         16

17   from process launch through shutdown and log flushing. Exact statistical postpro- 17
18   cessing took 8.204 seconds; process peak resident memory was 189128 KiB. Each                           18

19   arithmetic implementation evaluated 2621440 complete policy trajectories, and the                       19

20   additional rational midpoint check covered 2560 predetermined policy trajectories. 20
21   The recorded number of original-optimum value queries is zero. CPU frequency is                         21

22   observed where available, not claimed locked.                                                           22

23     The acquired-action rectangle tables are paid preprocessing, not a free neural                        23

24   operation. Their simple exhaustive construction is practical at the declared small                      24

25   covers but is not claimed to scale linearly in state dimension. The independent inte- 25
26   ger implementation deliberately pays for another complete calculation. The parent                       26

27   service also pays for reading and hashing controller records, both evaluations, exact                   27

28   midpoint checks, statistical inference, compression, durable output and shutdown. 28
29   These costs qualify the returned policies; they do not retime the original backward                     29

30   construction services or establish a new cold-start generator ranking.                                  30

31     Corollary 15 provides a priced decision for every declared deployment count                           31

32   and monetary scale. In particular, the gain interval in Table J.I yields an entire                      32
     102

1    replacement-fee frontier, rather than a favorable isolated fee. The zero-investment             1

2    incumbent is a feasible installed policy in the original economy. An improvement                2

3    over it does not establish dominance over an adaptive Bellman search or over every              3

4    conventional basis. The matched feature comparison and the retained conventional- 4
5    query catalogue must be read separately.                                                        5

6      The experiment’s units are those of the theoretical investment loss. A currency               6

7    calibration, an empirically estimated installation fee and a deployment-frequency               7

8    model would be additional economic assumptions. The mathematical adoption                       8

9    interval states their role explicitly and remains valid for all such nonnegative prices         9

10   on the same coverage event. It therefore supports a conditional purchase calculation            10

11   without manufacturing an empirical welfare estimate.                                            11

26   Use both estimators when their intervals belong to the joint family of Theorem 14. 26
27   Denote the resulting bounded set of cost vectors by C . An edge a → b of weight c               27

28   represents θb − θ a ≤ c, and d( a, b) is its shortest-path distance. These are classical        28

29   difference constraints; the computational reduction is not a new graph algorithm                29

30   (Floyd, 1962). Its role here is to turn paid NBO cost observations into an attainable,          30

31   price-uniform choice certificate.                                                               31

1    by [0.0758058, 0.0775680] for n = 8 and [0.0753723, 0.0771589] for n = 16, rounded                1

2    outward. These compare actual attained continuations and their complete feasible                  2

5      Let [l, u] be the closed interval for JReLU,8 − JReLU,16 . Suppose only the finer con- 5

11   The fine controller is universally optimal for f ≤ l; the coarse one is universally               11

12   optimal for f ≥ u. Between those thresholds, the regret-minimizing choice changes                 12

13   at (l + u)/2. Here [l, u] ⊂ [0.0002277, 0.0011901] and the exact switch is approxi- 13
14   mately 0.00070894. Exact endpoints, all adjacent gaps and attaining cost vectors                  14

15   are deposited. Transitive closure does not further tighten the paired bounds in this              15

16   particular catalogue; no such improvement is claimed.                                             16

17     For m deployments and monetary scale v > 0, replace f by the incremental                        17

18   total charge divided by mv. This gives a complete conditional fee region without                  18

19   assigning a monetary value to normalized losses or inferring a deployment horizon                 19

20   from the sample count. Acquiring finer information is an economic choice only after               20

18     The ordinary Floyd recurrence evaluates the all-pairs distances with (q + 1)3 can-      18

19   didate relaxations. The implementation uses exact fractions, including the rational       19

20   confidence endpoints, rather than shortest paths through rounded printed entries. 20
21   Its independent cross-check runs a separate single-source Bellman–Ford recurrence         21

22   from every vertex. Each returned attaining vector is checked against every original       22

23   edge and against every claimed attained difference. Negative self-loops and longer        23

24   negative cycles are regression cases; an inconsistent graph cannot pass by resetting      24

25   its diagonal.                                                                             25

29   [l, u], where these endpoints are the tight implications of C . Suppose all policies      29

31   inequalities identify B as less costly than every other controller in the catalogue, so   31

32   those controllers cannot be a best-cost comparator or a regret-minimizing choice. 32

5                                                                                                              5

25               AJ.1. Installed subsets rather than free simultaneous availability                25

26                                                                                                 26
       Suppose a decision maker serves m ≥ 1 deployments and may install a nonempty
27                                                                                                 27
     subset S at total normalized cost K (S). Write Ki = K ({i }), and assume K (S) ≥ Ki
28                                                                                                 28
     for every i ∈ S. The benchmark installs the best single controller, paying its own K j .
29                                                                                                 29
     For p supported on S, the exact worst-case average regret is
30                                                                                                 30
                                       (                           )
31                                                     K (S) − K j                                 31
                      RS ( p, w) = max ∑ pi Hji (w) +                .                      (67)
32                                   j    i
                                                           m                                       32
                            NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                           109

1    Indeed, the installation term is independent of θ, and the same attaining potential        1

2    proves the formula. The assumption on K makes the cost of an allocation at least           2

3    the weighted average of its corresponding singleton costs, and hence at least their        3

4    minimum; the regret is nonnegative. For each subset, (67) is a linear program on           4

5    ∆S . Enumerating the nonempty subsets and retaining the least optimum yields the           5

6    exact installation-and-allocation decision. It requires at most 2q − 1 programs and        6

7    is not advertised as dimension-free optimization. Lower bounds from the dual of            7

8    every subset, together with an attaining primal allocation for the chosen subset,          8

9    certify a global solution. A controller outside the installed subset remains a possible    9

10   oracle comparator and must not be removed from the rows.                                   10

12                   AJ.2. Two-controller formula and extra setup threshold                     12

13                                                                                              13
       With only the fine controller charged f , put a = f − l and b = u − f . Within the
14                                                                                              14
     ambiguity region both are positive and a + b = u − l. If p is the fine share, the two
15                                                                                              15
     worst-case comparator regrets are pa and (1 − p)b. Their maximum is minimized at
16                                                                                              16
     their intersection, giving p = b/( a + b) and value ab/( a + b). Outside that region
17                                                                                              17
     a uniformly optimal pure controller has zero regret. If l = u, the gain is known
18                                                                                              18
     exactly: use the fine controller below l, the coarse one above it, and any allocation
19                                                                                              19
     at equality; no division by a zero width is needed.
20                                                                                              20
       When both controllers incur additional cost κ only for 0 < p < 1, their optimal
21                                                                                              21
     nontrivial allocation is unchanged and its value is ab/( a + b) + κ. The best pure
22                                                                                              22
     value is min{ a, b}. Subtracting gives the exact saving min{ a, b}2 /( a + b) and proves
23                                                                                              23
     (48). Equality gives a tie. A positive extra cost can make the pure rule optimal;
24                                                                                              24
     the deployment analysis never assumes that loading or operating an additional
25                                                                                              25
     controller has zero cost. Formula (67) handles unequal singleton installation charges
26                                                                                              26
     as well.
27                                                                                              27

28                                                                                              28
                        AJ.3. A deterministic allocation to a finite cohort
29                                                                                              29

30     Random assignment is unnecessary when m homogeneous deployments with                     30

31   the same expected cost law can be assigned in advance. Deploy A on exactly k               31

32   tasks and B on the others, without choosing tasks according to unqualified outcome         32
     110

1    information. The average expected cost is the mixture with p = k/m; independence                              1

2    between tasks is not needed for this expectation identity. For l < f < u, a minimizing                        2

3    integer k is one of ⌊mp∗A ⌋ and ⌈mp∗A ⌉. This follows because the piecewise-linear                            3

4    regret decreases up to p∗A and increases afterwards. Nearest rounding also supplies                           4

5                                                                                                                  5
                                                         max{ f − l, u − f }
6                               0 ≤ Vm ( f ) − V ( f ) ≤                     .                                     6
                                                                2m
7                                                                                                                  7
     An extra two-controller setup charge is included only for 0 < k < m; both pure
8                                                                                                                  8
     endpoints must then also be tested. Nonhomogeneous deployment laws require
9                                                                                                                  9
     separately qualified cost vectors and are not covered by relabeling the existing
10                                                                                                                 10
     sample as a cohort.

5                                                                                               5
        APPENDIX AK: N EURAL B ELLMAN QUERIES WITHOUT A STATE LATTICE
6                                                                                               6

7      The preceding construction makes numerical error accountable, but a complete             7

8    tensor reference can absorb the benefit of a fitted continuation. We therefore change      8

9    the computational organization, not the economic problem. A Neural Bellman                 9

10   selector proposes an action at the state actually acquired. A recursive interval query     10

11   then bounds the original Bellman value at that state and checks the proposed               11

12   action against the entire continuous feasible interval. No conventional policy is          12

13   precomputed at all states. The controller is the resulting callable algorithm, not the     13

14   unverified proposal alone.                                                                 14

15     The distinction matters for attribution. Learning may reduce the work needed             15

16   to locate a useful action. It does not supply the inequalities used to certify that        16

17   action. The adaptive conventional comparator receives the same inequalities and            17

18   the same original-optimum target. The trained value-fit policies in the preceding          18

19   study remain distinct from the action-query predictor introduced here.                     19

20                                                                                              20

14     At an acquired state, retain an ordered partition of the feasible action interval and   14

15   an outward value bracket at every partition point. The minimum of the parabolic           15

16   minorants bounds the continuous-action infimum from below. The smallest upper             16

17   endpoint supplies an actual feasible witness. A neural or polynomial proposal             17

18   inserts another point into this partition; it cannot remove the endpoints or any part     18

19   of the feasible interval. The running lower bound is the maximum of all previously        19

20   valid global lower bounds, so new information cannot weaken the certificate. The          20

21   upper bound is the minimum over every evaluated feasible witness.                         21

7    that guard is a precision failure, not a successful return. The terminal query uses                        7

     114

1    The proof is in Supplement, Section 2.7.                                                  1

2      The finite-precision contract is explicit. The executed service uses the width guard,   2

3    a 512-point cap, and a 32-bit downward action lattice. A precision or cap failure         3

4    withholds the floating certificate and invokes a separately counted exact-rational        4

5    enumeration. This fallback constructs values only at the required descendants; it         5

6    does not load a precomputed conventional policy. The rational fallback chooses            6

7    dyadic action and quadrature counts until each cover or quadrature allowance is           7

8    at most one quarter of the request, and requests child width at most one quarter          8

9    divided by the discount. Provided δ > 8Λr 2−32 at every recursive level, its exact        9

10   brackets have width at most three quarters of the request. This proves a finite           10

11   return for every acquired state in the declared tasks, including states absent from       11

12   the workload. Rational bit-operation cost is additional work and is not replaced          12

13   by a unit-cost floating clock. The mathematical point budget is checked against           13

14   the declared tolerance range; termination in an arbitrary floating implementation         14

15   is not inferred merely from the real-arithmetic argument. Fitting convergence is          15

16   unnecessary for validity: any finite prediction, and even its clipped replacement         16

17   when numerically invalid, is only a proposed query.                                       17

                        AK.4. Work, prediction, and the economic object
14                                                                                           14

15
       Let Ar (δ) be a bound on action endpoints at a query. Excluding training, a scalar    15

16
     operation account obeys                                                                 16

17                                                                                           17
              W1 (δ) = O(dA1 ),      Wr (δ) ≤ CdAr (δ) + Ar (δ)qr Wr−1 (δ/(4β)).
18                                                                                           18

19   The constants dGr , dMr are bounded independently of d, so the declared endpoint        19

20   and quadrature budgets need not grow exponentially with the state dimension. 20
21   Primitive evaluations still cost O(d). This removes the state lattice, not the hori- 21
22   zon or shock-tree cost. The executed vectorization batches a query’s innovation         22

23   descendants. Its largest live state batch is reported; it does not claim the smaller    23

24   memory of a fully streamed implementation. Increasing the horizon or the shock di- 24
25   mension may still be expensive. The new execution is scalar-control; the earlier full   25

26   two-control result is retained rather than relabeled as a query-service experiment.     26

27     A useful prediction can reduce the endpoint count or return its own action            27

28   from the initial partition. An inaccurate prediction can instead add work. This is a    28

29   prediction-dependent algorithm, not a theorem of neural dominance. Learned warm         29

30   starts have their own literature, including Sakaue and Oki (2023); their discrete- 30
31   convex setting differs from the original-law Bellman intervals here. Convex control     31

32   policies and learned optimization parameters are studied by Agrawal et al. (2020). 32
     116

1    Our comparison identifies the extra proposal cost and the resulting query transcript           1

2    rather than claiming priority for learning a warm start.                                       2

3      The purchase is now a complete callable controller with a declared error contract. 3
4    Its training cost, every subsequent certified query, and its return-time cost belong to        4

5    the same service. A reduction in queried state storage is economically relevant for            5

6    repeated decisions only after these costs are counted. The original cost normalization         6

7    is maintained. Workload seconds and normalized expected-cost loss are reported                 7

8    separately; a price per unit of computation can convert the former to the latter only          8

9    as an explicitly specified decision-maker parameter.                                           9

11      APPENDIX AL: O PERATIONAL PREDICTION CERTIFICATES AND READOUT                               11

12                                            TRAINING                                              12

13     Proposition 21 separates witness accuracy from lower-certificate localization. Its           13

14   distance to an unknown minimizer is useful for analysis but is not a stopping                  14

15   observable. We now replace that distance by an upper envelope computed from                    15

16   information already purchased. This gives a certificate before an additional Bell- 16
17   man query, a training criterion measured in unresolved verification work, and a                17

18   constructive neural readout with a computable optimization gap.                                18

17   This is the quadratic H ∥ a∥22 /2 + b⊤ a + c interpolating the lower endpoints. Its                17

18   approximate minimizer is only a proposed support point. For any point y ∈ R2 ,                     18

19   including a numerically displaced point outside the triangle,                                      19

20                                                                                                      20
                          p(y) + min ∇ p(y)⊤ (v j − y) ≤              min            p ( a ).   (81)
21                                   j                           a∈co(v0 ,v1 ,v2 )                      21

22                                                                                                      22
     Thus an outward supporting-plane calculation, not an uncertified quadratic solver,
23                                                                                                      23
     supplies the lower endpoint.
24                                                                                                      24

25     T HEOREM 34—Simplicial Bellman query: For the two-control primitives, (80) is                    25

26   a lower minorant of the true Bellman objective. Taking the smallest valid triangle lower           26

27   endpoint and the best feasible-action upper endpoint gives a lower–upper certificate for           27

28   the full continuous simplex. Include the omitted capacity strip and actual action-rounding         28

29   allowances. Returning when this gap is at most δ gives the same acquired-policy loss               29

30   recursion as Theorem 30.                                                                           30

31     A failed floating triangulation can be replaced by an exact-rational simplex enumeration.        31

32   Under the stated positive precision slack, this recovery is finite at every finite horizon. With   32
                             NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                121

1    two innovations divided into q bins each, a valid local quadrature error is                      1

                                                  5βMt+1 d
                                             ζ=            .                                  (82)

                                                  24576q2                                             3

     If Ar bounds the number of action queries at a state with r dates remaining, a work account
5                                                                                                     5
     has the form
6                                                                                                     6

7                            Wr ≤ Cd Ar + Ar q2r Wr−1 ,        Cd = O(d),                     (83)    7

11   Proof. See Supplement, Section 3.4.                                                              11

12     The executed algorithm uses longest-edge refinement of the lowest triangle                     12

13   and an outward terminal convexity-gap check. Its floating cap is followed by the                 13

14   stated recovery rule, not by an uncertified returned action. The implementation                  14

15   batches descendants but can allocate a descendant array before slicing it into query             15

16   batches; a reported maximum query batch is therefore not a bound on total live                   16

17   state memory. We report process memory separately. The two-control execution is a                17

18   verified extension of the NBO query backend, not evidence for a trained two-output               18

19   selector.                                                                                        19

23                                                                                            23
     each expected polynomial feature by integrating its expanded monomials through
24                                                                                            24
                                       p +1           p +1
                                     v    −u

27   and each hinge by the integral of its positive-part antiderivative. This differs from    27

28   the construction’s even-moment expansion. Immediate state costs cancel in an action      28

29   contrast, leaving b2 − a2 + 4(b4 − a4 ) and the discounted expected-feature difference. 29
30   Every coefficient, primitive and multiplier in this replay is rational. The verifier     30

31   neither imports the construction’s LP routines nor calls its feature-expectation or      31

1                                AN.2. First-disagreement extension                                      1

2                                                                                                        2
       Let two finite-horizon policies have a common terminal cost and common tran-
3                                                                                                        3
     sition primitives. Suppose they choose the same action outside measurable sets
4                                                                                                        4
     Et at each date. Assume a common-innovation realization of the actual kernels is
5                                                                                                        5
     available, so trajectories agree while states and actions agree. Let the tail cash cost
6                                                                                                        6
     from date t lie in [mt , Mt ] under either policy, uniformly over feasible paths, and
7                                                                                                        7
     write Ht = Mt − mt . Put b0 = 1 and bt = ∏ j<t β j .
8                                                                                                        8

9                                                                                                        9
       P ROPOSITION 35—First-disagreement cost bound: If π
                                                         b is the reference policy and
10                                                                                                       10
     Dt is the event that the coupled policies first choose different actions at date t, then
11                                                                                                       11

12                                   T −1                 T −1                                           12

13
                       | J π − J πb | ≤ ∑ bt Ht Pr( Dt ) ≤ ∑ bt Ht Pr( Xt ∈ Et ).                 (84)   13
                                     t =0                 t =0       π
                                                                     b
14                                                                                                       14

15   The first events are disjoint. Any independent bound on the entire cash-cost difference may         15

16   be intersected with this bound. To apply it to an optimization class, the action agreement          16

17   and bad sets must hold uniformly for every policy in that class.                                    17

18                                                                                                       18

19     P ROOF : On Dt , the histories, states and costs before t coincide. The absolute                  19

20   difference of the remaining discounted costs is at most bt Ht . On the event of                     20

21   no disagreement the costs coincide throughout. Summing over the disjoint first- 21
22   disagreement events and taking expectations proves the first inequality. Up to the                  22

23   first disagreement, the common state is the state of the reference policy driven by                 23

24   the same innovations. A first disagreement at date t requires this state to lie in Et ,             24

25   proving the second inequality.                                                             Q.E.D.   25

26                                                                                                       26

27     This proposition is a cost-transfer identity, not a substitute for constructing longer- 27
28   horizon stable sublevels. In particular, changing a terminal readout changes labels                 28

29   upstream. Independently small optimization gaps at different fitted label prob- 29
30   lems do not automatically establish the uniform action-agreement premise. In the                    30

31   executed two-date experiment the last-date actor depends only on the common                         31

32   terminal payoff; Theorem 5 therefore needs the first-date switch problems alone.                    32
     124

1                         AN.3. Confidence and economic interpretation                           1

2                                                                                                2
       The original cost event concerns a finite list of frozen attained policies. On that
3                                                                                                3
     event all deterministic stability enlargements are valid simultaneously, including
4                                                                                                4
     for a coefficient vector selected after seeing the records but still belonging to a
5                                                                                                5
     certified fixed-label sublevel. The probability statement does not change because
6                                                                                                6
     the optimizer class is uncountable. A new unqualified model or newly fitted label
7                                                                                                7
     problem is not covered by that argument.
8                                                                                                8
       A failed action-stability certificate is a numerical statement about the available
9                                                                                                9
     lower bound, not a witness of policy variation. Conversely, policy invariance of
10                                                                                               10
     an exact optimization class does not assert that an arbitrary practical optimizer
11                                                                                               11
     reaches that class. The deposited feasible iterate and dual gap describe what the
12                                                                                               12
     construction attained; the switch lower bounds describe what every member of a
13                                                                                               13
     stated sublevel must do. These are distinct quantifiers, and neither is inferred from
14                                                                                               14
     a collection of fitting seeds.
15                                                                                               15
       For the two calibrated n = 16 controllers let [l, u] be the positive interval for
16                                                                                               16
     ReLU cost minus Bernstein cost. Let κ be the additional per-deployment charge for
17                                                                                               17
     Bernstein, after all actual installation and computation charges have been converted
18                                                                                               18
     into the same normalized cost units. The corresponding net difference is [l − κ, u − κ ].
19                                                                                               19
     Bernstein is strictly less costly for every κ < l; ReLU is strictly less costly for every
20                                                                                               20
     κ > u; neither strict ordering is certified in the intermediate region. This is an exact
21                                                                                               21
     conditional price region, not a monetary calibration. It remains valid for the fully
22                                                                                               22
     action-stable optimizer classes, and it does not use construction times with different
23                                                                                               23
     service boundaries as interchangeable monetary prices.
24                                                                                               24

29     Theorem 6 separates two numerical tasks. The action-switch dual identifies             29

30   actions that cannot be selected by a qualified readout. Original-law integration         30

31   prices the actions not excluded by that dual. Neither calculation substitutes a fitted   31

32   critic for the cost of the implemented continuation policy.                              32
